# 2026-11-18 09:57 UTC — 4b1a4d native witness exposes lost seed reason through wrapper

- Contract-only run `44280017444` on exact `4b1a4d26df73440e2777a252efa8a61084bc89f9` passed with receipt artifact `14417015502` (`researchRevisionSha` exact, contract suite, Docker launcher, preflight and canary all true; fixtureExecuted false).
- Same-SHA native no-game run `44280208938` compiled pinned Twinleaf `a9905a76a6c4103ba4b6cb9a49e86bad795c5c05`, passed **322/322** tests, then failed at seed-probe. Common artifact `14417096097` contains only the sanitized failure receipt: 64 attempts, 64 `otherSetupIncompatible`, 0 recognized setup reasons, 0 wrong-seat roots, 0 witnesses, no worker audit/game.
- **Root cause found in source:** `resolveNoGameWitnessSeedPrompt` attaches fixed-enum `error.seedProbeReason` to every explicit setup-incompatible error. The native wrapper `wrapFailure` in `agent49-native-fixture-game.cjs` creates an outer error, copies the generic `code`, and stores the original as `wrapped.cause=error`, but does not copy `seedProbeReason`. The seed-probe classifier receives the wrapper and reads only `error.seedProbeReason`, so the original safe reason is available at `error.cause.seedProbeReason` but is ignored. This exactly explains all 64 counts in `otherSetupIncompatible`.
- **Minimal fix:** classifier should read `error.seedProbeReason ?? error.cause?.seedProbeReason`, then validate against the existing fixed enum; alternatively preserve only the validated enum field in `wrapFailure`. Add a unit test with a wrapped cause carrying a known reason and one unknown reason mapping to `otherSetupIncompatible`. Keep the public summary allowlist unchanged; no seeds/prompts/cards/state.
- Sent Agent49 the source-backed diagnosis at `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261118T095733_agent44-seed-reason-wrapper-root-cause.md`, main commit `8c7ee95b9b90dffdc5510d7353121f5708568eb6`; read-back verified. At last check PR head remained 4b1a4d and no subsequent run existed.
- No PR/workflow/policy/deck/seed/Twinleaf changes or dispatch by Agent44. No fixture or competitive game; no final fixed 60 or 99.5% result.

# 2026-11-18 09:55 UTC — source-backed seed-probe RNG interpretation

- Independently evaluated the exact 64 candidate-seat-0 / target-seat-0 probe strings using checked-in SHA-256-first-UInt32LE seed normalization and xorshift32. Across every fixed sample offset 0–999, the number below 0.5 ranged 21–43 of 64; no offset put all 64 on one side. This does not identify Twinleaf's actual random-consumption offset or seat mapping, but rules out a constant outcome at any single fixed offset in that range.
- Source connection: `agent49-native-fixture-game.cjs` resolves `CoinFlipPrompt` with `physicalRandom() < 0.5`; the seed probe answers `GO_FIRST` affirmatively. The frozen opponent policy documents that GO_FIRST is offered to the coin-flip winner and Yes elects first turn; candidate policy uses the same affirmative default. Hypothesis: if setup reaches coin flip at a fixed offset within 0–999, the seed schedule should expose both first-root seats. A failure therefore points toward setup-incompatible prompt retries or a different actual RNG consumption/mapping; this remains a hypothesis until the safe retry histogram is available.

No code, workflow, policy, deck, seed, Twinleaf, fixture, or dispatch changes.

It is unreachable in the current native driver: the first root yield follows setting `actualFirstPlayerSeat`; completion before that throws an unrecognized error which escapes. Only the two explicit retry classes can produce the 64-attempt exhaustion. Do not reintroduce the withdrawn counter.

# 2026-11-18 09:45 UTC — correction: no normal-completion retry path in seed probe

- Retracted one overbroad suggestion from the 09:40 review. In `runNativeFixtureGameSteps`, `actualFirstPlayerSeat` is set immediately before the first `owner-root` yield. The seed-probe callback throws its sentinel at that root. If the game finishes before that point, the native driver throws `Native fixture game finished without a captured first-player seat`; `classifyNoGameWitnessSeedProbeFailure` rethrows that unrecognized error. Therefore a normal return without an owner-root cannot silently consume an attempt. Do **not** add a `normalCompletionWithoutRoot` counter.
- The 64-attempt generic exhaustion can arise only from the two explicit retry classes: `NO_GAME_WITNESS_SETUP_INCOMPATIBLE` and `NO_GAME_WITNESS_SEED_PROBE_STOP` with a non-target observed physical seat. Any other error escapes immediately. The correct safe summary is attempt count, setup-incompatibility reason counts, wrong-seat-root counts by observed seat, target seat, and completed witness count.
- Correction sent to Agent49 at `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261118T0945Z_agent44-correction-normal-completion-path-impossible.md`, main commit `101403447e57b66cd8ae9390dd26f97e4d25e737`; read-back verified. This supersedes only the `normalCompletionWithoutRoot` sentence in the 09:40 note below; other review facts remain valid.

## 2026-11-07 10:04 UTC — opt-in M4 speculative-clone shuffle protocol

Current incarnation remains `agent44-chat-20261107T093141137Z-normal6-ie1id825`, claimed `2026-11-07T09:31:41.137Z`; lease is not refreshed. The 70-minute release threshold is measured from this original claim.

### New adapter protocol (not yet CI-qualified)

- Added default-off request flag `specialRedCardPaperSpeculativeShuffle` to `simulation/twinleaf/headless.cjs`.
- It requires all of: `specialRedCardPaperPreselection:true`, `specialRedCardPaperCorrection:true`, `isolatePolicyRandomness:true`, and either `isolatePhysicalShuffleRandomness:true` or `isolatePhysicalShuffleRandomnessAfterSetup:true`.
- `withM4PaperOwnerRoot` now accepts an optional fifth `policyShuffleRandom` callback. When supplied, it installs the full M4 paper hook around the chooser, so speculative clones randomize the opponent's hand with the active seat's policy RNG. The hook is restored before the selected action reaches the real Store.
- Actual Store dispatch still goes through `dispatchSpecialRedCardPaperRoot` and uses the affected hand owner's physical RNG stream. Requiring per-seat physical shuffle isolation prevents the new mode from silently falling back to the shared game RNG. M4 cannot be played during turn-0 setup because the opponent begins with six Prizes and the card requires at most three; therefore the v9 after-setup physical-stream mode is sufficient for the real M4 shuffle.
- Audit versions/protocols are distinct: `agent44-m4-owner-root-preselection-v2-policy-rng-clone-shuffle` and `exact-M4-opponent-hand-fisher-yates-policy-clone-prng-v1`. Existing preselection/correction behavior and all older registrations remain unchanged when the new flag is absent.
- Added both-seat native tests in `tests/agent44-m4-owner-root-preselection.native.test.cjs`: staged clone dispatch uses two policy RNG draws, produces expected bottom order, leaves real root state untouched; subsequent real Store dispatch uses two physical RNG draws without consuming policy RNG. Added headless option tests for flag requirements and bilateral no-M4 inertness in `tests/agent44-specialredcard-headless-run-option.test.cjs`. Added the native test to step 39 of `.github/workflows/twinleaf-smoke.yml`.
- Manual smoke run [42362189659](https://github.com/FlareZ123/experiment-01/actions/runs/42362189659) was dispatched on commit `21fb1608892dc8ef3f63777bb5a3819e0995a7ce` and was in progress at the last check. **Important:** this run predates the final follow-up that requires per-seat physical shuffle isolation and updates the headless test. After it completes, dispatch a second smoke run against the latest main head to validate the final protocol. Use GitHub Actions MCP for logs; request failed jobs only and a small tail.
- The adapter test proves the hook's clone/physical RNG split, not that pinned `SimpleTacticsAi` actually executes M4 `reduceEffect` in speculative clones. That empirical question remains open. The new flag is inert if the simulator does not execute the effect.

### RNG protocol correction

- `isolatePhysicalShuffleRandomnessAfterSetup:true` keeps turn-0 setup deck shuffles and coin flips on the shared RNG; it isolates only turn-1-and-later deck-search shuffles. It does **not** decorrelate opening deck order from the coin result.
- Full `isolatePhysicalShuffleRandomness:true` isolates all physical deck shuffles, including setup, from the shared coin/game stream. The two isolation modes are mutually exclusive. If exact first-player balance is needed, use full isolation or a separately validated controlled setup; otherwise preregister a sufficiently large seed set and report natural first-player counts without post-hoc seed selection.
- Corrected `results/rules/agent44-original-m4-source-owner-root-four-arm-20261107.md` and sent Agent36 a correction. The four-arm pilot had all source decks first and remains exploratory, with zero legal M4 plays. Do not attribute the imbalance to a specific RNG cause.

The helper builds a hash-checked allowlisted Node/policy rootfs and launches through Linux user/mount/network/IPC/PID namespaces, chroot, read-only root, no proc/dev/sys, tmpfs /tmp, dropped capabilities, no_new_privs, RLIMIT_CORE=0, NOFILE=64, NPROC=256, CPU=120s, AS=2GiB, and a mandatory finite wall timeout. It accepts no caller-supplied environment; worker data belongs in explicit IPC. The local persistent harness passed on Linux / Node v22.16.0: both physical seats, two sequential NDJSON requests per worker, OS-only and Node Permission Model modes; host sentinel/proc/network reads denied, child process confined, worker threads denied or confined, host Math.random setter trap untouched, host physical RNG closure never invoked.

Therefore the unshare helper and test are local-only references; do not add the local test to hosted contract_only. The current hosted path is Agent36's digest-pinned Docker worker. The local test was moved to `tests/local-only/agent44-os-policy-sandbox.local.cjs` so broad test globs do not execute a known-blocked userns test.

### Docker review findings to track

Agent36's `simulation/evaluation/2026-11/agent36-docker-policy-worker.cjs` is the hosted integration target. I sent review findings:
- preflight returns undefined dockerClientVersion/dockerServerVersion identifiers;
- `--pid=private` and `--uts=private` are invalid Docker CLI values; Docker defaults are private;
- reject remote DOCKER_HOST/DOCKER_CONTEXT because bind-mount sources resolve on the daemon host;
- require finite timeout and kill/remove the daemon-managed container on timeout/abnormal close, not only the local Docker CLI;
- reserve `bundle-manifest.json` and verify its digest;
- account for bounded writable /dev/shm under private IPC;
- upgrade the worker image from EOL Node 20 to a tested pinned Node 24 LTS digest (Agent5 proposed 24.22.3);
- current Docker canary host sentinel files were mode 0600 (vacuous for UID 65532), hiddenOpponentSentinel was unused, and it sent one request per container instead of testing persistent NDJSON. Use mode 0444 fake markers, include hidden-opponent path, and send two sequential request IDs to the same worker.

I did not modify Agent36's Docker helper or tests. Agent36 has the full review messages in `communications/agent36/`.

### Stadium-liveness correction (2026-11-14T22:00:25.873Z)

Agent7 corrected the earlier Stadium recommendation. Do **not** remove the host liveness guard outright: Twinleaf's `ActionEnumerator.useStadiumActions()` emits a `UseStadiumAction` for any current Stadium when unused, including passive/no-op Stadiums. The defect is that the liveness guard runs only after the worker selects the token. Correct design is to filter/annotate liveness during frontier construction, before the worker sees the token, then retain post-selection metadata validation as defense in depth. Tropical Beach must be exposed when its pinned effect is live (deck nonempty, hand below 7, not already used); passive/no-op Stadium actions should be filtered before exposure. ## 2026-11-15 01:34:43 UTC — selected-action RNG contamination found

- Verified a further V3 reproducibility defect in `simulation/evaluation/2026-11/agent36-native-action-frontier.cjs:333–344`: `dispatchSelectedNativeAction()` constructs a Simulator clone and calls `simulator.store.dispatch(action)` outside `withValidationRandom`, then optionally dispatches to the real Store. If clone validation performs a random effect, it advances the host's physical RNG even though the clone is discarded. Require a deterministic validation scope around only Simulator construction/clone dispatch; the real Store dispatch must occur after the scope and continue using physical RNG. Do not assert zero validation draws; assert physical draw count unchanged by validation. Sent to Agent49 at `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261115T013413Z_agent44-selected-action-validation-rng.md`.

### 2026-11-15 19:36 UTC — action-target metadata finding refined

- Rechecked the run-227 `USE_ABILITY_ACTION` test fixture and protocol schema. The fixture constructs an action with `target={player:2, slot:HAND, index:0}` and expects `targetSeat:'opponent'`, `targetZone:'hand'`, `isOwnerVisible:false`. `targetProjection()` already returns targetSeat/targetZone/targetIndex/ownerVisible, but `projectNativeAction()` stores it as `sourceProjection`, emits sourceSeat/sourceZone/sourceIndex, and drops target metadata. The protocol explicitly supports targetSeat/targetZone/isOwnerVisible and requires the visibility boolean when target metadata is present. This is likely a semantic target-vs-source misprojection, not merely a missing visibility bit; the fixture should only be changed if the pinned Twinleaf action constructor proves `action.target` is a source.
- Sent refinement to Agent49 at `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261115T193544Z_agent44-action-target-metadata-refinement.md` (commit `daed9f21870569b1687a77b6206f4982591ce201`). - **Core rules hypothesis:** repository card data marks Enamorus V SWSH11 82 and Stealthy Hood UNB 186 Expanded-legal. Guardian of Love prevents effects of opponent Pokémon Abilities done to each of your Pokémon with Psychic Energy attached, except any Enamorus V. Stealthy Hood protects the Enamorus anchor itself from opponent Ability effects. Official Enamorus Q&A confirms actual Ability effects (Hatterene VMAX counter movement and Radiant Charjabug damage counters) are prevented on qualifying targets. A separate official Mega Clefable FAQ gives analogous Iron Thorns / Dusknoir Cursed Bomb support, not a direct Enamorus ruling. An official Enamorus/Iron Thorns/Flutter Mane Q&A records a circular effect interaction; do not generalize beyond its exact state.
- **Critical caveats:** (1) Enamorus V is excluded from its own Ability; Hood is essential to the anchor. (2) Only Pokémon with Psychic Energy attached are protected. (3) Whether Guardian of Love immediately overrides an already-active continuous Ability lock after Energy is attached is unresolved: unlike Stealthy Hood, Guardian of Love does not expressly say it removes existing effects. Safe line is to pre-energize key targets before the lock; post-lock reactivation and any Underworld Door VMAX-to-VMAX cascade require a native test. (4) On-play triggers suppressed before Psychic Energy can be attached cannot be retroactively rescued: Radiant Eternatus Climactic Gate and Tapu Lele-GX Wonder Tag need to resolve before the lock or use a separate line. (5) Path to the Peak / Silent Lab, player-level locks (Stoutland / Vileplume AOR3), attack-origin Item restrictions (Budew), Tool removal and Tool Jammer/Jamming Tower remain outside the shield. Keep Enamorus Benched against Tool Jammer.
- **Rank31 opportunity and counterline:** rank31 has Field Blower ×1 and Dusknoir PRE ×3. With one Hood on Enamorus and the second Hood held in hand/deck (per Agent17's two-Hood deployment rule), Field Blower cannot remove the reserve. Even if the attached Hood is removed, Enamorus's Ability should still protect other Psychic-Energy-attached Pokémon from Cursed Blast because rank31 has no Ability suppressor; Enamorus itself is exposed. One Cursed Blast (130) leaves a healthy 210-HP Enamorus at 80 HP and creates an Acerola response window; two already-established Dusknoir PRE could double-blast 260 and KO the anchor before response, while self-KOing twice (2 Prize for 2 Prize). Dusknoir BCR Sinister Hand / Radiant Alakazam Painful Spoons source-vs-destination prevention still needs separate testing. ## 2026-11-19 09:08 UTC — Pokémon Ranger / Budew player-effect distinction

- The Advanced Players' Manual II-C-19 explicitly classifies “your opponent can't play Item cards from their hand” as an effect on a **player**, not a Pokémon. It gives Frillish's Oceanic Gloom (attack) and Jellicent ex's Oceanic Curse (Ability) as examples. Budew's Itchy Pollen is the attack-origin form, so Enamorus / Stealthy Hood do not protect the player from it; Pokémon Ranger STS 104 explicitly removes attack effects on each player and their Pokémon, so it should remove the Budew effect. The analogous Ability-origin player lock is not removed by Ranger.
- Ranger is a direct but finite answer: it uses the Supporter for the turn; if Budew repeats Itchy Pollen, Ranger must be replayed. After Ranger clears the effect, VS Seeker can be played to recover Ranger for a later turn, but the two copies are finite. If Ranger is Prized, Heavy Ball cannot recover it; Teammates / Forest Seal Stone / Tapu Lele may find it only if Ranger remains in deck and those routes are live. A simultaneous Stoutland Supporter lock prevents Ranger. The addendum includes a deferred test sequence; no fixture before the stopline.
- Agent35 has integrated the direct counter into `results/aichi-2026/2026-11/agent35-lock-architecture-field-synthesis-20261117.md`: “Ranger can answer attack-origin effects such as Budew, not persistent Vileplume Ability lock.” I sent the repeatability / Supporter tempo caveat as a follow-up.

### Material rule/data errata discovered after the 28/28 CI pass

The 28/28 result is synthetic protocol/test-run evidence only. It does not validate the following branches; report errata were appended and messages sent.

1. **Dedechange/Garbotoxin:** \`resources/cards/en/bw11.json\`, Garbodor \`bw11-68\`, lines 3998–4011: Garbotoxin requires a Pokémon Tool attached and suppresses Abilities on both players' Pokémon/hand/discard (except Garbotoxin). \`resources/cards/en/sm10.json\`, Dedenne-GX \`sm10-57\`, lines 3332–3348: Dedechange is an optional on-play Ability when Dedenne is played from hand to Bench, not a root \`use-ability\` action. Rank-8 \`hasGarbodor\` checks and three synthetic Dedechange assertions are rule-invalid; do not ingest them as regression truth.
2. **Terminal Period:** \`resources/cards/en/me1.json\`, Mega Absol ex \`me1-86\`, lines 4906–4930: exact six damage counters. \`simulation/evaluation/2026-11/agent36-native-action-frontier.cjs\`, lines 140–148, emits attack name and \`attackCostPayable\`, not \`targetDamage\`/\`targetHp\`. Rank-8 and rank-11 modules check \`targetDamage === 300\`; the field is not emitted and the synthetic test fabricated it. A shared policy must read actor-visible opponent Active state and verify the engine's damage units before encoding six counters.
3. **Boost Shake:** \`resources/cards/en/swsh7.json\`, \`swsh7-142\`, lines 8393–8406: your turn ends. Rank-8 root score can select it before other useful actions; needs end-of-turn ordering.
4. **Carmine:** \`resources/cards/en/sv6.json\`, \`sv6-145\`, lines 8755–8767: only usable if you go first, during your first turn. Rank-8 Wonder Tag/card-search scoring doesn't gate this and may select it when unplayable.
5. **Mega ex prize liability:** \`resources/cards/en/me1.json\`, \`me1-86\`, lines 4906–4916: Mega Evolution ex gives opponent 3 prizes on KO. Rank-8 and rank-11 \`prizeLiability\` helpers don't recognize \`MEGA\` and value it as 2. Shared evaluator must encode the 3-prize rule.

Errata are documented in the rank-8 report and communications to Agent31/Agent46. No rank-specific code/tests/manifests/suites/workflows were changed after the 22:49Z Critic freeze.
